The Saeima has adopted and the President of State proclaims the following law:

Criminal Law

GENERAL PART

## I nodaļa

General Provisions

### 1.pants. Basis for Criminal Liability

(1) Only a person who is guilty of committing a criminal offence may be held criminally liable and punished, that is, a person who has intentionally or through negligence committed an offence provided for in this law, which has all the elements of a criminal offence.

(2) A person may be found guilty of committing a criminal offence and a criminal punishment may be imposed by a court judgement and in accordance with law.

(3) In cases provided for by law, a person is also found guilty of committing a criminal offence and punishment is determined by a prosecutor, by drawing up a penal order.

(4) An act may not be recognised as criminal by applying the law by analogy.

(5) No one may be tried or punished again for an offence for which he or she has already been acquitted or punished by a decision in a criminal case or administrative offence case that has been adopted and entered into force in the procedure provided for by law. This does not exclude re-examination of a case in accordance with law if newly discovered circumstances have been established or if a substantial violation of substantive or procedural law has been committed in the previous proceedings, which could have affected the outcome of the case.

(With amendments made by the laws of 28.09.2005., 13.12.2012. and 15.05.2014., which entered into force on 14.06.2014.)

### 2.pants. Force of the Criminal Law in the Territory of Latvia

(1) A person who has committed a criminal offence in the territory of Latvia is liable in accordance with this law.

(2) If a criminal offence in the territory of Latvia has been committed by a foreign diplomatic representative or another person who, in accordance with laws in force or international treaties binding on the Republic of Latvia, is not subject to the jurisdiction of the Republic of Latvia, the question of bringing this person to criminal liability shall be decided through diplomatic channels or in accordance with mutual agreement between states.

### 3.pants. Force of the Criminal Law on Aircraft, Sea and River Vessels Outside the Territory of Latvia

A person who has committed a criminal offence outside the territory of Latvia while on an aircraft, sea or river vessel or other floating craft, if this craft is registered in the Republic of Latvia and if international treaties binding on the Republic of Latvia do not provide otherwise, shall be brought to liability in accordance with this law.

### 4.pants. Force of the Criminal Law Outside the Territory of Latvia

(1) Citizens of Latvia, non-citizens or foreigners who have a permanent residence permit in the Republic of Latvia, for an offence committed in the territory of another state or outside the territory of any state, regardless of whether it is recognised as criminal and punishable at the place of commission, shall be brought to liability in the territory of Latvia in accordance with this law.

(11) For an offence committed in the territory of another state or outside the territory of any state, regardless of whether it is recognised as criminal and punishable at the place of commission, if it has been committed by a natural person in the interests of, for the benefit of, or as a result of inadequate supervision or control of a legal person registered in the Republic of Latvia, coercive measures provided for in this law may be applied to the legal person.

(2) Service members of the Republic of Latvia who are deployed outside the territory of Latvia shall be brought to liability for criminal offences in accordance with this law, unless international treaties binding on the Republic of Latvia provide otherwise.

(3) Foreigners who do not have a permanent residence permit in the Republic of Latvia and who have committed serious or particularly serious crimes in the territory of another state that are directed against the interests of the Republic of Latvia or its inhabitants, regardless of the laws of the state in whose territory the crime was committed, shall be brought to criminal liability in accordance with this law if they have not been brought to criminal liability or brought to trial in accordance with the laws of the state where the crime was committed.

(4) Foreigners who do not have a permanent residence permit in the Republic of Latvia and who have committed a criminal offence in the territory of another state or outside the territory of any state, regardless of the laws of the state where the offence was committed, shall be brought to liability in accordance with this law in cases provided for in international treaties binding on the Republic of Latvia, if they have not been brought to criminal liability or brought to trial for this offence in the territory of another state.

(With amendments made by the laws of 17.10.2002., 16.12.2004., 21.05.2009., 21.10.2010. and 25.09.2014., which entered into force on 29.10.2014.)

### 5.pants. Force of the Criminal Law in Time

(1) The criminality and punishability of an offence (action or omission) is determined by the law that was in force at the time of commission of this offence.

(2) A law that recognises an offence as not punishable, mitigates punishment or is otherwise favourable to a person, unless the respective law provides otherwise, has retroactive force, namely, it applies to offences committed before the entry into force of the respective law, as well as to a person who is serving a sentence or has served a sentence but for whom punishability has been retained.

(3) A law that recognises an offence as punishable, increases punishment or is otherwise unfavourable to a person does not have retroactive force.

(4) A person who has committed a crime against humanity, a crime against peace, a war crime and participated in genocide is punishable regardless of the time of commission of these crimes.

## II nodaļa

Criminal Offence

### 6.pants. Concept of Criminal Offence

(1) A criminal offence is recognised as a harmful offence (action or omission) committed with intent (intentionally) or through negligence, which is provided for in this law and for the commission of which a criminal punishment is threatened.

(2) An offence (action or omission) that has the elements of an offence provided for in this law but which was committed in circumstances that exclude criminal liability is not recognised as criminal.

(With amendments made by the law of 13.12.2012., which entered into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 7.pants. Classification of Criminal Offences

(1) According to the nature and harmfulness of the threat to the interests of a person or society, criminal offences are divided into criminal violations and crimes. Crimes are divided as follows: less serious crimes, serious crimes and particularly serious crimes.

(2) A criminal violation is an offence for which this law provides deprivation of liberty for a period from fifteen days but not longer than three months (short-term deprivation of liberty), or a lighter type of punishment.

(3) A less serious crime is an intentional offence for which this law provides deprivation of liberty for a period longer than three months but not longer than three years, as well as an offence committed through negligence and for which this law provides deprivation of liberty for a period not longer than eight years.

(4) A serious crime is an intentional offence for which this law provides deprivation of liberty for a period longer than three years but not longer than eight years, as well as an offence committed through negligence and for which this law provides deprivation of liberty for a period longer than eight years.

(5) A particularly serious crime is an intentional offence for which this law provides deprivation of liberty for a period longer than eight years, or life imprisonment.

(6) If for a crime this law provides deprivation of liberty for a period not longer than five years, then for the respective crime it also provides a lighter type of punishment.

(With amendments made by the laws of 21.05.2009., 01.12.2011., 13.12.2012. and 17.12.2020., which entered into force

### 8.pants. Forms of Guilt

(1) Only a person who has committed it with intent (intentionally) or through negligence is recognised as guilty of a criminal offence.

(2) When determining the form of guilt of the person who committed the criminal offence, the mental attitude of this person towards the objective elements of the criminal offence must be established.

### 9.pants. Commission of a Criminal Offence with Intent (Intentionally)

(1) A criminal offence is recognised as committed with intent (intentionally) if the person committed it with direct or indirect intent.

(2) A criminal offence is recognised as committed with direct intent if the person was aware of the harmfulness of their action or omission and consciously performed or allowed it, or if the person was aware of the harmfulness of their action or omission, foresaw the harmful consequences of the offence and desired their occurrence.

(3) A criminal offence is recognised as committed with indirect intent if the person was aware of the harmfulness of their action or omission, foresaw the harmful consequences of the offence and, although they did not desire these consequences, consciously allowed their occurrence.

(In the wording of the law of 13.12.2012., which entered into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 10.pants. Commission of a Criminal Offense by Negligence

(1) A criminal offense is deemed to have been committed by negligence if a person has committed it through criminal overconfidence or criminal carelessness.

(2) A criminal offense is deemed to have been committed through criminal overconfidence if a person has foreseen the possibility of harmful consequences of their action or inaction occurring, but has frivolously relied on being able to prevent them.

(3) A criminal offense is deemed to have been committed through criminal carelessness if a person has not foreseen the possibility of harmful consequences of their action or inaction occurring, although according to the specific circumstances of the offense they should have and could have foreseen the said harmful consequences.

(4) An offense provided for in this law is not criminally punishable if a person did not foresee, should not have and could not have foreseen the possibility of harmful consequences of their action or inaction occurring.

(As amended by the law of 13.12.2012, which comes into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 11.pants. Age at which Criminal Liability Arises

A natural person who has reached the age of fourteen by the day of commission of a criminal offense is subject to criminal liability. A minor, that is, a person who has not reached the age of fourteen, is not subject to criminal liability.

### 12.pants. Liability of a Natural Person in a Legal Person's Case

For a criminal offense committed by a natural person in the interests of a private law legal person, for the benefit of that person or as a result of its improper supervision or control, the respective natural person is subject to criminal liability, and coercive measures provided for in this law may be applied to the legal person.

(As amended by the law of 14.03.2013, which comes into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 13.pants. Non-Imputability

(1) A person who at the time of commission of the offense was in a state of non-imputability, that is, due to mental disorders or mental retardation could not understand their actions or control them, is not subject to criminal liability.

(2) To a person who has been recognized as non-imputable, the court may apply compulsory measures of a medical nature specified in this law.

(With amendments made by the law of 20.03.2014, which comes into force on 03.04.2014.)

### 14.pants. Diminished Imputability

(1) If a person at the time of commission of a criminal offense due to mental disorders or mental retardation has not been able to fully understand their actions or control them, that is, was in a state of diminished imputability, depending on the specific circumstances of the offense, the court may mitigate the sentence to be imposed or release this person from punishment.

(2) To a person who has been recognized as having diminished imputability, the court applies compulsory measures of a medical nature specified in this law.

### 15.pants. Completed and Uncompleted Criminal Offense

(1) A criminal offense is deemed to be completed if it has all the elements of a criminal offense provided for in this law.

(2) Preparation for a crime and an attempt of a crime are uncompleted criminal offenses.

(3) Preparation for a crime is deemed to be the search for or adaptation of means or instruments or other intentional creation of favorable circumstances for the commission of an intentional crime, if moreover it has not been continued due to reasons independent of the will of the guilty person. Criminal liability arises only for preparation for serious or particularly serious crimes.

(4) An attempt of a crime is deemed to be a deliberate action (inaction) directly aimed at its intentional commission, if the crime has not been completed due to reasons independent of the will of the guilty person.

(5) Liability for preparation for a crime or an attempt of a crime arises in accordance with the same section of this law that provides for liability for the specific offense.

(6) For an attempt to commit a misdemeanor, a person is not subject to criminal liability.

### 16.pants. Voluntary Renunciation

(1) Voluntary renunciation of the commission of a criminal offense is the complete cessation of a criminal offense initiated by a person by the will of that person, with the person being aware that there is a possibility to complete the criminal offense.

(2) A person who has voluntarily renounced the commission of a criminal offense is not subject to criminal liability. Such a person is liable only if the offense actually committed by them contains elements of another criminal offense.

### 17.pants. Perpetrator of a Criminal Offense

A perpetrator of a criminal offense is deemed to be a person who has directly committed the criminal offense themselves or has used another person in its commission who, in accordance with the provisions of this law, is not subject to criminal liability.

### 18.pants. Participation of Several Persons in a Criminal Offense

Joint deliberate participation of two or more persons in the commission of an intentional criminal offense is co-perpetration or complicity.

### 19.pants. Co-perpetration

Co-perpetration (joint commission) is deemed to be a deliberate criminal act by which two or more persons (that is, a group) have jointly and knowingly directly committed an intentional criminal offense. Each of these persons is a participant (co-perpetrator) of the criminal offense.

### 20.pants. Complicity

(1) Complicity is deemed to be a deliberate action or inaction by which a person (accomplice) has participated jointly with another person (perpetrator) in the commission of an intentional criminal offense, but has not been its direct perpetrator. Accomplices of a criminal offense are organizers, inciters, and abettors.

(2) An organizer is deemed to be a person who has organized or directed the commission of a criminal offense.

(3) An inciter is deemed to be a person who has incited another person to commit a criminal offense.

(4) An abettor is deemed to be a person who has knowingly facilitated the commission of a criminal offense by giving advice, instructions, means or by removing obstacles to its commission, as well as a person who has promised in advance to conceal the perpetrator or accomplice of a criminal offense, the instruments and means of commission of the criminal offense, the traces of the criminal offense or objects obtained by criminal means, or has promised in advance to acquire or dispose of such objects.

(5) An accomplice in criminal offenses is subject to liability in accordance with the same section of this law that provides for the perpetrator's liability.

(6) Individual elements of a criminal offense that relate to the perpetrator or accomplice of a criminal offense do not affect the liability of other co-perpetrators or accomplices.

(7) If an accomplice was not aware of any of the criminal offenses committed by the perpetrator and other accomplices, they are not subject to criminal liability for them.

(8) If the perpetrator has not completed the crime due to reasons independent of their will, the accomplices are liable for complicity in the attempt of the respective crime. If the perpetrator has not commenced the commission of the crime, the accomplices are liable for preparation for the respective crime.

(9) Voluntary renunciation by an organizer and an inciter from completing the commission of a criminal offense is deemed to be such only in cases where they have timely done everything possible to prevent the commission of the criminal offense intended with their complicity, and this offense has not been committed. An abettor is not subject to criminal liability if they have voluntarily refused to provide the promised assistance before the commencement of the criminal offense.

### 21.pants. Organized Group

(1) An organized group is an association formed by more than two persons, which has been created for the purpose of jointly committing one or more crimes and whose members have divided duties pursuant to a prior agreement.

(2) Liability for commission of a crime in an organized group arises for a person in the cases provided for in this law for the formation and management of a group, participation in the preparation of a serious or particularly serious crime or commission of a crime, regardless of the person's role in the jointly committed offense.

(With amendments made by the laws of 25.04.2002 and 13.12.2012, which comes into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 22.pants. Concealment Without Prior Promise and Failure to Report

(1) Concealment without prior promise of a perpetrator or participant of a crime, as well as of the instruments and means of committing a crime or the traces of a crime, and failure to report a crime does not constitute participation, and criminal liability therefor arises only in the cases provided for in this Law.

(2) A betrothed person, spouse, parents, children, brothers and sisters, grandparents and grandchildren of the person who committed a crime, as well as a person with whom the natural person who committed the crime lives together and with whom they have a common (undivided) household, shall not be held liable for concealment without prior promise and failure to report.

(3) In cases provided for by law, other persons shall also not be held liable for failure to report.

(With amendments made by the laws of 25.09.2014 and 06.06.2019, which enter into force on 03.07.2019.)

### 23.pants. Separate (Single) Criminal Offence

(1) A separate (single) criminal offence is one act (action or omission) which has the elements of one criminal offence, or two or more interrelated criminal offences which are covered by the single intent of the guilty person and which correspond to the elements of only one criminal offence.

(2) Continued and continuing criminal offences also constitute a separate (single) criminal offence.

(3) A separate continued criminal offence consists of several interrelated criminal actions of the same kind directed towards a common objective, if they are covered by the single intent of the guilty person, and therefore in their totality they constitute one criminal offence.

(4) A separate continuing criminal offence is the continuous commission of the elements of one criminal offence (action or omission) which is associated with the subsequent continuing non-performance of those obligations which the law has imposed on the guilty person under threat of criminal prosecution.

(5) (Deleted by the law of 13.12.2012)

(With amendments made by the law of 13.12.2012, which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 24.pants. Plurality of Criminal Offences

(1) Plurality of criminal offences exists when one person commits (or allows) two or more independent acts (action or omission) which correspond to the elements of several criminal offences, or when a person commits (or allows) one act (action or omission) which corresponds to the elements of at least two different criminal offences.

(2) Plurality of criminal offences is constituted by an aggregate of criminal offences and recidivism.

(3) Plurality of criminal offences is also constituted by such criminal offences for which a sentence imposed abroad is being executed in Latvia.

(With amendments made by the laws of 20.06.2002 and 13.12.2012, which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 25.pants. Repetition of Criminal Offences

(Deleted by the law of 13.12.2012, which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 26.pants. Aggregate of Criminal Offences

(1) An aggregate of criminal offences is constituted by one act or several acts committed by one person which correspond to the elements of two or more criminal offences, if this person has not been convicted for any of these criminal offences and the statute of limitations for criminal liability has not expired.

(2) An act committed by a person which corresponds to the elements of several different interrelated criminal offences constitutes an ideal aggregate of criminal offences.

(3) Two or more unrelated acts committed by a person which correspond to the elements of several criminal offences constitute a real aggregate of criminal offences.

(4) An aggregate of criminal offences is not constituted by an act for the commission of which a person has been released from criminal liability.

(5) If one criminal offence corresponds to the general and special norm provided for in the Special Part of this Law, then an aggregate of criminal offences does not arise and criminal liability arises only in accordance with the special norm.

(With amendments made by the law of 13.12.2012, which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 27.pants. Recidivism of Criminal Offences

Recidivism of a criminal offence is constituted by a new intentional criminal offence committed by a person after this person's conviction for a previously committed intentional criminal offence, if the conviction therefor has not been withdrawn or expunged in the manner prescribed by law at the time of committing the new criminal offence.

(In the version of the law of 12.11.2015, which enters into force on 02.12.2015.)

## Chapter III

Circumstances Excluding Criminal Liability

### 28.pants. Types of Circumstances Excluding Criminal Liability

Circumstances which exclude criminal liability, even though the actions committed in these circumstances correspond to the elements of a criminal offence provided for in this Law, are necessary defence, apprehension causing harm to a person, extreme necessity, justified professional risk and execution of a criminal order or criminal instruction.

### 29.pants. Necessary Defence

(1) Necessary defence is an action committed in defence of the interests of the state or society, one's own or another person's rights, as well as a person against an attack or threat of attack in such a way that harm is caused to the attacker. Criminal liability for this action arises if the limits of necessary defence have been exceeded.

(2) Exceeding the limits of necessary defence means an obvious disproportion of the defence with the nature and danger of the attack, as a result of which harm is caused to the attacker which was not necessary to prevent or repel the attack.

(3) Causing harm to an attacker through negligence when repelling an attack is not criminally punishable.

(4) A person has the right to necessary defence regardless of the possibility to avoid the attack or to seek assistance from other persons.

### 30.pants. Putative Defence

(1) Putative defence exists when the real attack referred to in Section 29 of this Law does not occur, but the person mistakenly believes that such an attack is occurring.

(2) In cases where the circumstances of the incident have given grounds to assume that a real attack is occurring, but the person who applied defensive measures has not been aware that such an assumption is mistaken, and moreover could not and should not have been aware of it, the actions of this person shall be assessed as necessary defence.

(3) A person who has exceeded the limits of defence that would be permissible in the circumstances of a corresponding real attack shall be held liable as for exceeding the limits of necessary defence.

(4) A person who causes harm corresponding to the elements of a criminal offence to the putative attacker without being aware of the putative nature of the attack, although in the specific circumstances they should have and could have been aware of it, shall be held liable for the respective act as one committed through negligence.

### 31.pants. Apprehension Causing Harm to a Person

(1) Apprehension causing harm to a person is an action directed against a person who is committing or has committed a criminal offence. Criminal liability for this action does not arise if there has been no obvious disproportion of the harm caused to the person with the nature of the act, non-compliance or resistance.

(2) A person who, when carrying out apprehension, has violated the conditions of apprehension shall be held liable for this violation of conditions.

(3) If the actions by which harm was caused to the person to be apprehended were not necessary for their apprehension, liability for the harm caused arises on general grounds.

(4) Causing harm to a person to be apprehended through negligence is not criminally punishable.

### 32.pants. Extreme Necessity

Extreme necessity is an action which a person commits to prevent harm that threatens the interests of the state or society, the rights of this person or another person, as well as these or other persons, if the respective harm could not have been prevented by other means in the specific circumstances and if the harm caused is less than the harm prevented. Extreme necessity excludes criminal liability.

### 33.pants. Justifiable Professional Risk

(1) Criminal liability does not arise for harm caused by professional activity that has elements of a criminal offense if this activity was performed to achieve a socially beneficial purpose that could not be achieved in another way. The professional risk associated with this activity is considered justifiable if the person who allowed the risk has done everything to prevent harm to legally protected interests.

(2) Risk shall not be recognized as justified if it is knowingly associated with a threat to the lives of several persons or with threats to cause an environmental disaster or public calamity.

### 34.pants. Execution of a Criminal Order and Criminal Instruction

(1) The execution of a criminal order or criminal instruction by the person who executed it is justifiable only in cases where this person did not realize the criminal nature of the order or instruction and it was not obvious. Criminal liability in such cases nevertheless arises when crimes against humanity and peace, war crimes and genocide have been committed.

(2) A person who has not executed a criminal order or instruction shall not be held criminally liable.

## IV nodaļa

Punishment

### 35.pants. Punishment and its Purpose

(1) The punishment provided for in the Criminal Law is a coercive measure which, within the framework of this law, is imposed in the name of the state by a court on a person guilty of committing a criminal offense, or in cases provided for by law, is determined by a prosecutor by drawing up a penal order.

(2) The purpose of punishment is:

- 1) to protect public safety;
- 2) to restore justice;
- 3) to punish the guilty person for the criminal offense committed;
- 4) to resocialize the convicted person;
- 5) to ensure that the convicted person and other persons comply with laws and refrain from committing criminal offenses.

(With amendments made by the laws of 28.09.2005 and 13.12.2012, which entered into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 36.pants. Types of Punishments

(1) A person who has committed a criminal offense may be sentenced to one of the following principal punishments:

- 1) (deleted by the law of 01.12.2011);
- 2) deprivation of liberty;
- 3) (deleted by the law of 13.12.2012);
- 4) (deleted by the law of 13.12.2012);
- 41) probation supervision;
- 5) community service;
- 6) fine.
  - (2) In addition to the principal punishment, the convicted person may be sentenced to the following supplementary punishments:
- 1) confiscation of property;
- 2) expulsion from the Republic of Latvia;
- 3) probation supervision;
- 4) community service;
- 5) fine;
- 6) restriction of rights.
  - (3) For a person who has committed a criminal violation, a less serious crime or a serious crime for which a sentence of deprivation of liberty of up to five years is provided, the prosecutor, by drawing up a penal order, may impose probation supervision, community service or a fine, as well as supplementary punishments — community service, fine or restriction of rights.
  - (4) The procedure for serving punishments is determined in accordance with law.

(With amendments made by the laws of 12.02.2004, 28.09.2005, 08.12.2005, 21.05.2009, 08.07.2011, 01.12.2011, 13.12.2012, 0.03.2016 and 17.12.2020, which entered into force on 01.01.2022.)

### 37.pants. Death Penalty

(Deleted by the law of 01.12.2011, which entered into force on 01.01.2012.)

### 38.pants. Deprivation of Liberty

(1) Deprivation of liberty is the forced detention of a person in imprisonment.

(2) Deprivation of liberty is imposed for a period from fifteen days to twenty years.

(21) In cases specifically provided for in this law for criminal violations and crimes for which this law provides for deprivation of liberty for a period not exceeding five years, short-term deprivation of liberty may be imposed for a period of up to three months.

(3) In cases specifically provided for in this law, deprivation of liberty may be imposed for life (life imprisonment).

(4) The period of deprivation of liberty shall be determined in years, months and days.

(With amendments made by the laws of 16.06.2009, 13.12.2012, 25.09.2014 and 12.11.2015, which entered into force 38.1 pants. Probation Supervision

(1) Probation supervision as a principal punishment or supplementary punishment is the forced involvement of a person in social behavior correction and social rehabilitation measures appropriate to their age, psychological characteristics and level of development.

(2) Probation supervision as a principal punishment may be imposed only in the cases mentioned in the Special Part of this law for a period from one year to five years.

(3) Probation supervision as a supplementary punishment may be imposed only together with deprivation of liberty in the cases mentioned in the Special Part of this law for a period from one year to three years. In cases specifically provided for in the Special Part of this law, probation supervision as a supplementary punishment may also be imposed on a person for a longer period, but not exceeding five years. In accordance with the nature of the criminal offense, the court may also impose probation supervision as a supplementary punishment in cases where this punishment is not provided for in the sanction of the relevant article of the Special Part of this law, for a period from one year to three years.

(4) During the probation supervision period, the convicted person or the person for whom probation supervision has been imposed by a prosecutor's penal order shall fulfill the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal sentences and imposed by the sentence execution institution.

(5) The execution of probation supervision as a supplementary punishment shall commence after serving the sentence of deprivation of liberty. If a person is conditionally released before the expiration of the term from serving the sentence of deprivation of liberty, the execution of probation supervision as a supplementary punishment shall commence from the moment when the supervision of the person after conditional early release has ended.

(6) Upon receipt of a submission from the sentence execution institution, the court may reduce the term of probation supervision or cancel probation supervision if the person for whom probation supervision has been imposed by a court judgment or prosecutor's penal order has served half of the imposed sentence term, has exemplarily fulfilled the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal sentences and imposed by the sentence execution institution, and has cooperated with the sentence execution institution in resolving identified problems of a criminogenic nature and achieving socially supportable objectives.

(7) If a person for whom probation supervision has been imposed by a court judgment or prosecutor's penal order fails to fulfill the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal sentences or imposed by the sentence execution institution without a valid reason, upon receipt of a submission from the sentence execution institution, the court shall replace the unserved sentence term by counting one day of probation supervision as one day of deprivation of liberty, but not exceeding:

- 1) three months if probation supervision was imposed for a criminal violation;
- 2) three years if probation supervision was imposed for a less serious crime;
- 3) five years if probation supervision was imposed for a serious or particularly serious crime.

(As worded by the law of 17.12.2020, with amendments made by the law of 07.04.2022, which entered into force on 04.05.2022.)

### 39.pants. Arrest

(Deleted by the law of 13.12.2012, which entered into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 40. pants. Community Service

(1) Community service as a principal or supplementary punishment is the forced involvement of a person in work necessary for society, appropriate to their age, psychological characteristics, physical abilities and level of development, during time free from main work or studies and without remuneration.

(2) Community service as a principal punishment shall be imposed for a period from forty to two hundred eighty hours. Community service as a supplementary punishment for a period from forty to one hundred hours may be applied to a person who has been sentenced conditionally or for whom probation supervision has been imposed as a principal punishment.

(3) Community service is not applicable to persons who are unable to perform it due to physical or mental disorders.

(4) Upon the proposal of the sentence execution institution, the court may release from serving the sentence a person who has been sentenced to community service or for whom community service has been imposed by prosecutor's penal order, if the community service has been imposed for a period not less than eighty hours, if at least half of the imposed sentence has actually been served and the person has fulfilled the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal punishments, has observed the conditions and procedure for the execution of community service, and has also performed the community service in an exemplary manner.

(5) If a person who has been sentenced to community service, or a person for whom community service has been imposed by prosecutor's penal order, fails to perform it without justifiable reason, the court, upon receipt of a submission from the sentence execution institution, shall replace the unserved sentence with short-term deprivation of liberty, calculating four work hours as one day of short-term deprivation of liberty, but not longer than three months.

(In the wording of the law of 17.12.2020 with amendments made by the law of 07.04.2022, which enters into force on 04.05.2022.)

### 41.pants. Fine

(1) A fine as a principal or supplementary punishment is a sum of money which the court or prosecutor imposes to be paid in favor of the state within 30 days in the amounts specified in this Section.

(2) A fine as a principal punishment, according to the harmfulness of the criminal offence and the financial situation of the guilty person, shall be imposed:

- 1) for a criminal violation — in the amount of one to one hundred minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;
- 2) for a less serious crime — in the amount of three to one thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;
- 3) for a serious crime for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a term not exceeding five years — in the amount of ten to two thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia.
  - (21) A fine shall be imposed in the amount of minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia as it is on the day of rendering the judgment, indicating in the judgment the amount of this fine in the monetary units of the Republic of Latvia.
  - (22) According to the harmfulness of the criminal offence and the financial situation of the guilty person, the court may also impose a fine as a principal punishment for the commission of a serious crime for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a term exceeding five years, and for the commission of a particularly serious crime, if the crime has not caused a person's death, has not caused serious bodily injury to at least one person or less serious bodily injury to several persons, is not connected with violence or threat of violence, is not connected with illegal circulation of narcotic and psychotropic substances and has not been committed in an organized group, in the amount of three hundred to ten thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia.
  - (3) A fine as a supplementary punishment, according to the harmfulness of the criminal offence and the financial situation of the guilty person, shall be imposed:
- 1) for a criminal violation — in the amount of one to one hundred minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;
- 2) for a less serious crime — in the amount of three to one thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;
- 3) for a serious crime for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a term not exceeding five years — in the amount of five to two thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia;
- 4) for a serious crime for which this Law provides deprivation of liberty for a term exceeding five years, and for a particularly serious crime — in the amount of ten to five thousand minimum monthly wages established in the Republic of Latvia.
  - (4) The financial situation of the guilty person shall be determined by assessing their ability to immediately pay the fine or to obtain foreseeable income that would allow them to pay the imposed fine within the time specified by law.
  - (5) The court or prosecutor, respectively, may divide the payment of the fine into installments or postpone it for a period not exceeding one year from the day when the judgment or penal order has entered into legal force. If the fine exceeds three hundred minimum monthly wages, the court or prosecutor, respectively, may divide its payment into installments or postpone it for a period not exceeding three years from the day when the judgment or penal order has entered into legal force.
  - (6) A fine not paid within the specified time, if it does not exceed thirty minimum monthly wages, shall be replaced with short-term deprivation of liberty, calculating one minimum monthly wage as four days of short-term deprivation of liberty, but not more than three months; an unpaid fine, if it does not exceed two hundred minimum monthly wages, shall be replaced with deprivation of liberty, calculating one minimum monthly wage as four days of deprivation of liberty, but not more than one year; an unpaid fine, if it exceeds two hundred minimum monthly wages, shall be replaced with deprivation of liberty, calculating one minimum monthly wage as five days of deprivation of liberty, but not more than five years.
  - (7) If the fine or part thereof is paid while the convicted person is serving a sentence of deprivation of liberty in place of the fine, they shall be released or the term of deprivation of liberty shall be reduced according to the part of the fine paid.

When reducing the term of punishment in the manner specified, the time of deprivation of liberty shall be counted according to the proportion determined by the court. (In the wording of the law of 16.06.2009 with amendments made by the laws of 13.12.2012., 12.11.2015., 29.10.2015., 10.03.2016., 08.06.2017., 06.06.2019., 11.06.2020., 06.07.2021. and 07.04.2022., which enters into force on 04.05.2022.)

### 42.pants. Confiscation of Property

(1) Confiscation of property is the forced uncompensated alienation of property in the ownership of the convicted person into state ownership. Confiscation of property may be imposed as a supplementary punishment. Property in the ownership of the convicted person that they have transferred to another natural or legal person may also be confiscated.

(2) Confiscation of property may be imposed only in cases provided for in the Special Part of this Law.

(3) The court, when imposing confiscation of property, shall specifically indicate what property is to be confiscated.

(4) The law specifies the property necessary for the convicted person or persons in their care that is not subject to confiscation.

(In the wording of the law of 12.02.2004 with amendments made by the laws of 06.10.2005., 21.05.2009., 13.12.2012. and 22.06.2017., which enters into force on 01.08.2017.)

### 43.pants. Expulsion from the Republic of Latvia

(1) A citizen of another state or a person who has a permanent residence permit in another state may be expelled from the Republic of Latvia if the court finds that, considering the circumstances of the case and the personality of the guilty person, his or her presence in the Republic of Latvia is not permissible.

(11) Expulsion from the Republic of Latvia is not applicable to a citizen of a European Union Member State, a European Economic Area state or the Swiss Confederation, as well as his or her spouse, children up to the age of twenty-one, parents or a person with whom he or she lives together and with whom he or she has a common (undivided) household or who is his or her dependent, except in cases where the court finds that, considering the circumstances of the case, the nature of the criminal offence and the personality of the guilty person, his or her presence in the Republic of Latvia poses a serious threat to public order or national security. If such a person has resided in the Republic of Latvia for the previous ten years or is a minor, then expulsion shall apply to him or her only if the court finds that, considering the circumstances of the case, the nature of the criminal offence and the personality of the guilty person, his or her presence in the Republic of Latvia poses a serious threat to national security. Expulsion from the Republic of Latvia of a minor shall also apply if it is in the interests of the minor in accordance with international treaties binding on the Republic of Latvia.

(2) This penalty may be imposed as an additional penalty together with deprivation of liberty or a fine, by establishing a prohibition on entry for a period of three to ten years and executing it after serving the principal penalty or after release from serving the penalty before the term in the manner prescribed by law, or after the judgment enters into force in the case of conditional conviction. The time of serving the additional penalty shall be counted from the day the person is expelled from the Republic of Latvia.

(As amended by the laws of 13.12.2007., 13.12.2012. and 16.10.2014., which enters into force on 01.02.2015.)

### 44.pants. Restriction of Rights

(1) Restriction of rights is the deprivation of certain rights or the establishment of a prohibition that prevents a person from exercising certain rights, holding a certain position, engaging in a certain professional or other type of activity, visiting certain places or events.

(2) Restriction of rights is an additional penalty imposed by a court or, when issuing a penal order, determined by a prosecutor for a period of one to five years, by depriving the rights mentioned in the relevant article of the Special Part of this law or by establishing a prohibition. Considering the type and nature of the criminal offence, in cases specifically provided for in the Special Part of this law, rights may also be restricted for a longer period, but not exceeding ten years.

(3) In accordance with the nature of the criminal offence, restriction of rights may also be established in cases where this penalty is not provided for in the sanction of the relevant article of the Special Part of this law, or in addition to the restriction of rights provided for in the sanction of the relevant article of the Special Part of this law, another restriction of rights may also be established.

(4) If a person is convicted with deprivation of liberty and with restriction of rights, then the prohibition mentioned in this article applies both to the time when the person is serving the deprivation of liberty, as well as to the time of serving the additional penalty specified in the judgment, counted from the day the person finishes serving the principal penalty. When establishing this additional penalty together with conditional deprivation of liberty, the time of serving the additional penalty shall be counted from the day the established probation period begins for the person. When establishing this additional penalty together with other types of principal penalties, the time of serving the additional penalty shall be counted from the day the conviction or the prosecutor's penal order enters into force.

(In the wording of the law of 13.12.2012. as amended by the laws of 12.11.2015., 10.03.2016. and 05.10.2023., which enters into force on 20.10.2023.)

44.1 pants. Prohibition to Stand as a Candidate in Elections to the Saeima, the European Parliament, City Councils of Cities of Republican Significance and Municipal Councils

(Deleted by the law of 13.12.2012., which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)

### 45.pants. Police Control

(Deleted by the law of 08.07.2011., which enters into force on 01.10.2011. The amendment on the deletion of the article enters into force

01.01.2015. See paragraph 10 of the transitional provisions)

45.1 pants. Probation Supervision

(Deleted by the law of 17.12.2020., which enters into force on 01.01.2022.)

## V nodaļa

Determination of Punishment

### 46.pants. General Principles of Determining Punishment

(1) The penalty is determined in the amount provided for the committed criminal offence by the sanction of the relevant article of the Special Part of this law, in compliance with the provisions of the General Part of this law.

(2) When determining the type of penalty, the nature and harm caused by the committed criminal offence, as well as the personality of the guilty person, shall be taken into account.

(3) When determining the measure of penalty, mitigating and aggravating circumstances shall be taken into account.

(31) If the sanction of an article of the Special Part of this law provides only one principal penalty for the committed crime — deprivation of liberty, when determining its duration, the nature of the criminal offence, the harm caused, the personality of the guilty person, as well as mitigating and aggravating circumstances shall be taken into account.

(4) The penalty of deprivation of liberty for a misdemeanour and a less serious crime shall be applied if the objective of the penalty cannot be achieved by determining one of the lighter types of penalty provided for in the sanction of the relevant article.

(In the wording of the law of 13.12.2012. as amended by the law of 07.04.2022., which enters into force on 04.05.2022.)

### 47.pants. Mitigating Circumstances

(1) The following circumstances shall be recognized as mitigating:

- 1) the perpetrator of the criminal offence came forward as guilty, sincerely confessed and regretted the act committed;
- 2) the guilty person actively facilitated the detection and investigation of the criminal offence;
- 3) the guilty person voluntarily compensated the victim for the harm caused by the criminal offence or eliminated the harm caused;
- 4) the guilty person facilitated the detection of another person's crime;
- 5) the criminal offence was committed under the influence of unlawful or immoral conduct of the victim;
- 6) the criminal offence was committed in violation of the conditions of lawfulness of necessary defence, extreme necessity, apprehension of the person who committed the criminal offence, justified professional risk, execution of an order and instruction;
- 7) the criminal offence was committed by a person in a state of diminished responsibility.
  - (2) When determining punishment, another circumstance related to the committed criminal offence that is not provided for in this law may also be recognized as a mitigating circumstance.
  - (3) A circumstance that is provided for in this law as an element of the composition of a criminal offence shall not be recognized as a mitigating circumstance.

(As amended by the law of 13.12.2012., which enters into force on 01.04.2013.)


This excerpt sits under: Izdevējs: Saeima Publicēts: > IV nodaļa

### 48.pants. Aggravating circumstances

(1) The following circumstances may be recognized as aggravating:

- 1) the criminal offense constitutes recidivism of criminal offenses;
- 2) the criminal offense was committed by a group of persons;
- 3) the criminal offense was committed by abusing an official position or the trust of another person;
- 4) the criminal offense has caused serious consequences;
- 5) the criminal offense was committed against a woman, knowing that she is pregnant;
- 6) the criminal offense was committed against a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or against a person by taking advantage of their helpless condition or infirmity due to age;
- 7) the criminal offense was committed against a person by taking advantage of their official, financial or other dependence on the guilty person;
- 8) the criminal offense was committed with particular cruelty or by mocking the victim;
- 9) the criminal offense was committed by taking advantage of circumstances of public disaster or during a state of emergency or state of exception;
- 10) the criminal offense was committed using weapons or explosives or in another generally dangerous manner;
- 11) the criminal offense was committed out of mercenary motives;
- 12) the criminal offense was committed under the influence of alcohol, narcotic, psychotropic, toxic or other intoxicating substances;
- 13) the person who committed the criminal offense has intentionally provided false information about a criminal offense committed by another person in order to obtain a reduction of punishment;
- 14) a criminal offense was committed out of racist, national, ethnic or religious motives or out of social hatred;
- 15) a criminal offense related to violence or threat of violence, or a criminal offense against morals and sexual inviolability was committed against a person with whom the offender is related in the first or second degree of kinship, or against a spouse or former spouse, or against a person with whom the offender is or has been in a permanent intimate relationship, or against a person with whom the offender has a common (undivided) household;
- 16) a criminal offense related to violence or threat of violence, or an intentional criminal offense against a person's health or morals and sexual inviolability was committed in the presence of a minor;
- 17) the person who committed the criminal offense has provided intentionally false testimony.
  - (2) Taking into account the nature of the criminal offense, any of the circumstances specified in paragraph one of this Section may not be recognized as aggravating.
  - (3) When imposing a punishment, circumstances that are not specified in this Law may not be recognized as aggravating.
  - (4) A circumstance that is provided for in this Law as an element of the composition of a criminal offense shall not be recognized as an aggravating circumstance.

(With amendments made by the laws of 27.05.2004., 12.10.2006., 21.10.2010., 13.12.2012., 15.05.2014., 25.09.2014., 08.06.2017., 26.04.2018., 11.06.2020. and 06.07.2021., which came into force on 05.08.2021.)

### 49.pants. Imposition of a lighter punishment than the punishment provided for by law

(1) If the court, taking into account several mitigating circumstances and the personality of the guilty person, considers it necessary to impose a punishment that is lower than the minimum limit provided for by law for the relevant criminal offense, it may mitigate the punishment accordingly, mandatorily indicating the reasons for such decision in the judgment.

(11) When imposing a custodial sentence that is lower than the minimum limit provided for the relevant criminal offense in the Special Part of this Law, a person may not be sentenced to less than:

- 1) one half of the minimum sentence provided for in the sanction of the relevant section for commission of a serious crime;
- 2) two thirds of the minimum sentence provided for in the sanction of the relevant section for commission of a particularly serious crime.
  - (2) The court, taking into account several mitigating circumstances and the personality of the guilty person, for the commission of a serious crime for which this Law provides for deprivation of liberty for a period longer than five years, and for the commission of a particularly serious crime, may impose a lighter type of punishment if the crime in question has not caused the death of a person, has not caused serious bodily injuries to at least one person or less serious bodily injuries to several persons, is not related to violence or threat of violence, is not related to the illegal circulation of narcotic and psychotropic substances and was not committed in an organized group.
  - (3) Paragraphs one and two of this Section shall not apply if the court has found that the criminal offense was committed in aggravating circumstances.
  - (4) (Deleted by the Law of 08.11.2007.) (With amendments made by the laws of 25.04.2002., 08.11.2007., 13.12.2012. and 08.06.2017., which came into force 49.1 pants. Imposition of punishment if the right to completion of criminal proceedings within a reasonable time has not been observed
  - (1) If the court finds that the person's right to completion of criminal proceedings within a reasonable time has not been observed, it may:
- 1) take this circumstance into account when imposing punishment and mitigate the punishment;
- 2) impose a punishment that is lower than the minimum limit provided for by law for the relevant criminal offense;
- 3) impose another, lighter type of punishment than provided for by law for the relevant criminal offense.
  - (2) If the court finds that the person's right to completion of criminal proceedings within a reasonable time has not been observed and the person has committed a crime for which life imprisonment is provided for in the sanction of a section of the Special Part of the Criminal Law, the court may impose deprivation of liberty for twenty years instead of life imprisonment.

(In the version of the Law of 21.10.2010. with amendments made by the Law of 01.12.2011., which came into force on 01.01.2012.)


This excerpt sits under: Izdevējs: Saeima Publicēts: > V nodaļa

### 50.pants. Determination of Punishment for Several Criminal Offences

(1) If a person has committed several independent criminal offences, the court, in rendering a judgment, or the prosecutor, in drafting a penalty order, determines the punishment separately for each criminal offence. In such case, the final punishment shall be determined according to the aggregate of criminal offences, by absorbing the lighter punishment into the heavier one or by fully or partially adding the imposed punishments.

(2) If all criminal offences constituting the aggregate of criminal offences are misdemeanours or less serious crimes, the final punishment shall be determined by absorbing the lighter punishment into the heavier one or by fully or partially adding the imposed punishments. In such case, the total amount or term of punishment may exceed the maximum amount or term of punishment provided for the most serious of the committed criminal offences, but not more than by half of the maximum amount or term of punishment provided for the most serious of the committed criminal offences.

(3) If at least one criminal offence constituting the aggregate of criminal offences is a serious or particularly serious crime, the final punishment shall be determined by fully or partially adding the imposed punishments. In such case, the total amount or term of punishment may exceed the maximum amount or term of punishment provided for the most serious of the committed criminal offences, but not more than by half of the maximum amount or term of punishment provided for the most serious of the committed criminal offences. The total term of added custodial sentences (except life imprisonment) may not exceed twenty-five years, but if a particularly serious crime has been committed as a result of which the death of the victim has occurred, the total term of custodial sentence may also be determined for the whole of life (life imprisonment).

(4) An additional punishment, like the principal punishment, shall first be determined separately for each criminal offence, but then according to the aggregate of criminal offences together with the principal punishment. To the principal punishment determined according to the aggregate of criminal offences are added the additional punishments imposed separately for each criminal offence.

(41) If the principal punishment and additional punishment correspond to one type of punishment, then they shall be fully or partially added, determining the final punishment as the principal punishment. In such case, the total amount or term of punishment may exceed the maximum amount or term provided for the respective type of principal punishment, but not more than by half of it.

(5) In the same manner the court determines the punishment if after the entry into force of a judgment or prosecutor's penalty order it is established that the person is guilty of another criminal offence committed before the entry into force of the judgment or prosecutor's penalty order in the first case. In this case, the punishment already fully or partially served under the first judgment or prosecutor's penalty order shall be included in the term of punishment. If the term of deprivation of liberty conditionally determined in the judgment is greater than the term of deprivation of liberty determined in another judgment, then the term of deprivation of liberty determined conditionally shall be fully or partially added to the term of deprivation of liberty.

(6) The total amount or term of the final punishment determined in the manner of this Section may exceed the maximum amount or term determined for the respective type of punishment.

(As amended by laws of 21.10.2010., 13.12.2012., 25.09.2014., 08.06.2017., 17.12.2020. and 07.04.2022., which came into force on 04.05.2022.)

### 51.pants. Determination of Punishment According to Several Judgments

(1) If a convicted person after the entry into force of a judgment but before full serving of the punishment has committed a new criminal offence, the court in the new judgment fully or partially adds to the determined punishment the punishment that has not been served under the previous judgment.

(11) When determining punishment according to several judgments, not less than one third of the unserved punishment shall be added to the punishment if:

- 1) a person who has been punished for a serious or particularly serious crime has committed a serious or particularly serious crime during the unserved term of punishment;
- 2) recidivism of criminal offences has been established;
- 3) the new criminal offence has been committed during the probation period determined by the court;
- 4) the new criminal offence has been committed during the time when the person has been conditionally released before the term from serving the principal punishment.
  - (2) The final punishment according to several judgments shall be greater than the punishment determined for the newly committed criminal offence, as well as than the part of the unserved punishment under the previous judgment.
  - (3) When adding punishments according to several judgments, the total amount or term of punishment may exceed the maximum amount or term determined for the respective type of punishment, but not more than by half of the maximum amount or term determined for the respective type of punishment. The total term of added custodial sentences (except life imprisonment) may not exceed thirty years, but if a particularly serious crime has been committed as a result of which the death of the victim has occurred, the total term of custodial sentence may also be determined for the whole of life (life imprisonment).
  - (31) If a person sentenced to life, who has been released from serving the punishment conditionally before the term, commits a criminal offence during the pre-term supervision, the court adds life imprisonment to the determined punishment.
  - (4) A judgment within the meaning of Sections 51 and 52 of this law is also a prosecutor's penalty order.

(As amended by laws of 13.12.2007., 21.10.2010., 13.12.2012., 25.09.2014. and 11.06.2020., which came into force on 06.07.2020.)

### 52.pants. Rules for Aggregating and Substituting Punishments

(1) When aggregating punishments for several criminal offences in one judgment or following several judgments, one day of deprivation of liberty corresponds to:

- 1) (deleted by law of 13.12.2012.);
- 2) eight hours of community service;
- 3) (deleted by law of 08.07.2011.);
- 4) two days of probation supervision.
  - (2) A fine, restriction of rights, if these punishments are imposed together with deprivation of liberty, probation supervision or community service, are executed independently. In the case of conditional conviction, the additional punishment — community service — is executed independently, starting from the probation period.
  - (21) If for several criminal offences in one judgment or following several judgments basic punishments have been imposed — community service and deprivation of liberty, the term of which together does not exceed five years —, then the punishment
    - community service — is executed independently. In such cases, the execution of community service begins after serving the punishment of deprivation of liberty, but in the case of conditional conviction — immediately after the judgment takes effect.
  - (22) If for several criminal offences in one judgment or following several judgments punishments have been imposed — community service and probation supervision —, then community service is executed concurrently with probation supervision.
  - (23) If for several criminal offences in one judgment or following several judgments basic punishments have been imposed — probation supervision and deprivation of liberty, which is not conditional and the term of which together does not exceed five years —, then the punishment — probation supervision — is executed independently. In such cases, the execution of probation supervision begins after serving the punishment of deprivation of liberty.
  - (3) When determining punishments not mentioned in the first part of this article, the court, taking into account detention, the served part of the punishment or the period of application of medical coercive measures, may mitigate the punishment or fully release the guilty person from serving the punishment.
  - (4) Terms of punishment are counted in years, months and days. The court counts detention in the term of punishment, counting one day of detention as one day of deprivation of liberty. The court counts arrest in the term of punishment, counting arrest up to twenty-four hours as one day of deprivation of liberty. Detention that coincides in time with serving a punishment of deprivation of liberty for another criminal offence is not counted in the term of punishment.
  - (5) Detention, arrest and the served part of the punishment are counted in the term of the more severe punishment in accordance with the provisions of the first part of this article.
  - (6) House arrest is counted in the term of deprivation of liberty. One day of house arrest corresponds to one day of deprivation of liberty.
  - (7) Substituted punishments are fully aggregated.

(With amendments made by laws of 12.06.2003., 28.09.2005., 16.06.2009., 08.07.2011., 13.12.2012., 15.05.2014., 08.06.2017., 06.06.2019., 11.06.2020. and 17.12.2020., which enter into force on 01.01.2022.)

### 53.pants. Determination of Punishment for Preparation for a Crime and for Attempt to Commit a Crime

When determining the punishment for preparation for a crime or for an attempt to commit a crime, the court takes into account the nature of the acts committed by the guilty person and the harm caused by them, the degree of realization of the criminal intent and the reasons why the crime was not completed.

### 54.pants. Determination of Punishment for a Criminal Offence Committed in Complicity

(1) When determining the punishment for participants in a criminal offence, the court takes into account the nature and role of each person's participation in the committed criminal offence.

(2) Circumstances aggravating and mitigating liability that relate to an individual participant are taken into account by the court when determining the punishment only for that participant.

### 55.pants. Conditional Conviction

(1) If, when determining the punishment — deprivation of liberty — longer than three months but not longer than five years, or not longer than three years if a particularly serious crime has been committed, the court, taking into account the nature of the committed criminal offence and the harm caused, the personality of the guilty person and other circumstances of the case, becomes convinced that the guilty person, without serving the punishment, will not commit further offences, it may convict the guilty person conditionally.

(2) In this case, the court decides not to execute the punishment if during the probation period it has set the convicted person does not commit a new criminal offence, does not violate public order and fulfils the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal punishments and established by the punishment execution institution.

(3) When convicting conditionally, the court sets a probation period from six months to five years. The probation period begins on the day the court judgment takes effect. The set probation period may not be less than the imposed term of deprivation of liberty.

(4) When convicting conditionally, the judgment must indicate the circumstances due to which the court has found it appropriate not to execute the punishment.

(5) When convicting conditionally, additional punishments may be imposed. The additional punishment — probation supervision — is executed only if the court decides to execute the basic punishment set in the judgment.

(6) (Deleted by law of 16.10.2014.)

(7) (Deleted by law of 16.10.2014.)

(8) (Deleted by law of 16.10.2014.)

(9) If the conditionally convicted person without justifiable reason does not fulfil the obligations provided for in the law regulating the execution of criminal punishments or established by the punishment execution institution, the court, based on the application of the punishment execution institution, may adopt a decision on the execution of the punishment set in the judgment for the convicted person or on the extension of the probation period up to one year.

(10) If the conditionally convicted person during the probation period commits a new criminal offence, the punishment imposed on him is subject to execution and the court determines the punishment in accordance with the provisions set forth in Articles 51 and 52 of this law.

(101) If the conditionally convicted person has committed a new criminal offence through negligence and for the new criminal offence he has been released from criminal liability in accordance with Article 58 of this law or released from punishment in accordance with Article 59 of this law, or for the new criminal offence a fine or community service has been set as the basic punishment for him, the court may adopt a decision on the extension of the probation period up to one year.

(11) Conditional conviction may not be applied to a person for committing an intentional crime if the person has previously been convicted with deprivation of liberty and the criminal record for that has not been removed or expunged in the manner prescribed by law. Conditional conviction may not be applied to a person who has committed a crime provided for in Article 159 or 160 of this law. (With amendments made by laws of 18.12.2003., 27.05.2004., 12.02.2004., 21.06.2007., 13.12.2007., 08.11.2007., 08.07.2011., 13.12.2012., 25.09.2014., 16.10.2014., 08.06.2017., 11.06.2020. and 17.12.220., which enter into force

## VI nodaļa

Release from Criminal Liability and Punishment

### 56.pants. Statute of Limitations for Criminal Liability

(1) A person may not be held criminally liable if the following period has elapsed since the day on which the person committed the criminal offence:

- 1) (repealed by law of 21.10.2010.);
- 2) two years from the day of commission of a criminal violation;
- 3) five years from the day of commission of a less serious crime;
- 4) ten years from the day of commission of a serious crime;
- 5) fifteen years from the day of commission of a particularly serious crime, except for a crime for which life imprisonment may be imposed in accordance with law;
- 6) (repealed by law of 12.11.2015.).
  - (11) A person may not be held criminally liable if twenty years have elapsed since the day on which the victim in a criminal offence directed against the morality and sexual inviolability of a minor or involving serious bodily injury associated with genital mutilation or loss of reproductive capacity, or in human trafficking, or in forcing to perform an abortion, reached eighteen years of age, except for a crime for which life imprisonment may be imposed in accordance with law.
  - (2) The limitation period shall be calculated from the day on which the criminal offence was committed, or in the case provided for in Paragraph 1.1 of this Section from the day on which the victim reached eighteen years of age, until the presentation of the indictment or notification of an official extradition request to the accused, if the accused is located in another country and a search has been announced for him or her.
  - (3) The limitation period shall be interrupted if, before the expiry of the periods specified in Paragraph one or 1.1 of this Section, the person who committed the criminal offence commits a new criminal offence. In this case, the limitation period provided for the most serious of the criminal offences committed shall start to run from the moment of commission of the new criminal offence.
  - (4) The question of the application of the statute of limitations to a person who has committed a crime for which life imprisonment may be imposed shall be decided by a court if thirty years have elapsed since the day of commission of the crime or since the day on which the victim of a crime directed against the morality and sexual inviolability of a minor reached eighteen years of age.

(With amendments made by laws of 20.05.2004., 28.09.2005., 12.10.2006., 21.10.2010., 01.12.2011., 13.12.2012., 12.11.2015. and 08.06.2017., which enters into force on 01.01.2018.)

### 57.pants. Non-Application of Statute of Limitations

A person who has committed a crime against humanity, a crime against peace, a war crime, or participated in genocide shall not be subject to a statute of limitations for criminal liability.

### 58.pants. Release from Criminal Liability

(1) A person who has committed an insignificant criminal offence may be released from criminal liability.

(2) A person who has committed a criminal violation or a less serious crime, except for criminal offences resulting in the death of a person, may be released from criminal liability if there is a reconciliation with the victim or his or her representative and the person has not been released from criminal liability in the last year for committing an intentional criminal offence by concluding a reconciliation, and has fully eliminated the harm caused by the criminal offence committed or compensated for the damage caused.

(3) An accused who has substantially assisted in the detection of a serious or particularly serious crime committed by another person, which is more serious or more dangerous than the criminal offence committed by the accused himself or herself, may be released from criminal liability if that other person has been held criminally liable for that crime. This provision does not apply to a person who is held criminally liable for committing a particularly serious crime provided for in Sections 116, 117, 118, 125, 159, 160, 176, 190.1, 251, 252 and 253.1 of this Law, or to a person who has created or led an organised group or gang himself or herself.

(4) A person may also be released from criminal liability in cases specifically provided for in the Special Part of this Law.

(5) A person may be released from criminal liability if it is established that his or her right to completion of criminal proceedings within a reasonable time has not been observed.

(6) A person may be released from criminal liability if he or she committed a criminal offence at a time when he or she was subject to human trafficking and was forced to commit it.

(With amendments made by laws of 25.04.2002., 28.09.2005., 21.05.2009., 21.10.2010., 13.12.2012., 11.06.2020. and 07.04.2022., which enters into force on 04.05.2022.)

58.1 pants. Conditional Release from Criminal Liability

(1) A person who has committed a criminal violation or a less serious crime and who has not been convicted of an intentional criminal offence, and against whom criminal proceedings have not been terminated in the last five years by conditional release from criminal liability, may be released from criminal liability conditionally by a prosecutor if, taking into account the nature of the offence and the harm caused, information characterising the accused and other circumstances of the case, it is established with conviction that the accused will not commit criminal offences in the future.

(11) A prosecutor may, in the manner prescribed by law, also conditionally release from criminal liability a person who is accused of committing a serious crime, who has not been convicted of an intentional criminal offence, and against whom criminal proceedings have not been terminated in the last five years by conditional release from criminal liability, and who has substantially assisted in the detection of a serious or particularly serious crime committed by another person, which is more serious or more dangerous than the criminal offence committed by the person himself or herself, if that other person has been held criminally liable for that crime. This provision does not apply to a person who is held criminally liable for committing a serious crime provided for in Sections 125, 159, 160, 176, 190.1, 251, 252 and 253.1 of this Law, or to a person who has been the organiser of the crime himself or herself.

(2) In conditionally releasing from criminal liability, the prosecutor decides not to continue the criminal prosecution of the person for this offence if during the probationary period the person does not commit a new criminal offence and fulfils the obligations imposed, as well as, if a reconciliation has been concluded, fulfils its conditions.

(3) In conditionally releasing from criminal liability, the prosecutor shall set a probationary period for the person from three to eighteen months. The probationary period shall begin on the day on which the decision of the prosecutor enters into force.

(4) In conditionally releasing from criminal liability, the prosecutor may, with the person's consent, impose an obligation:

- 1) to apologise to the victim;
- 2) to eliminate the harm caused within a specified period;
- 21) (repealed by law of 07.04.2022.);
- 3) to report periodically to the sentence enforcement institution and participate in probation programmes in accordance with the instructions of the sentence enforcement institution;
- 31) (repealed by law of 07.04.2022.);
- 4) to refrain from certain types of conduct or occupation;
- 5) to report periodically to the sentence enforcement institution and visit a specialist designated by that institution to carry out addiction diagnostics, and to follow the instructions of the specialist, including to undergo treatment for alcohol, narcotic, psychotropic, toxic substance or other addiction;
- 6) to pay a guarantee sum within a specified period.
  - (41) The amount of the guarantee sum shall be determined by the prosecutor, taking into account the nature of the criminal offence and the harm caused thereby, the financial situation of the person, as well as the type and measure of punishment provided for by law.
  - (5) If a person who has been conditionally released from criminal liability commits a new intentional criminal offence during the probationary period or fails to fulfil the obligations imposed or the conditions of reconciliation, his or her criminal prosecution shall be continued. In such cases, the guarantee sum paid shall be credited to the state budget.

(In the wording of the law of 20.06.2002. with amendments made by laws of 18.12.2003., 27.05.2004., 21.06.2007., 21.05.2009., 16.06.2009., 15.05.2014., 17.12.2020. and 07.04.2022., which enters into force on 04.05.2022. Point 6 of Paragraph four, Paragraph 4.1 and the amendment regarding the addition of a sentence to Paragraph five enter into force on 03.11.2022. See Transitional Provisions of the law of 07.04.2022.)


This excerpt sits under: Izdevējs: Saeima Publicēts: > VI nodaļa

### 59.pants. Release from Punishment or Service of Punishment

(1) The release of a convicted person from punishment or service of punishment, as well as mitigation of the imposed punishment, except for release from punishment or mitigation of punishment by way of amnesty and pardon, may be carried out only by a court in the cases and procedure specified by law.

(2) The court may release a minor from punishment in the cases provided for in this law by imposing compulsory measures of an educational nature.

(3) The court may also release a person from punishment in the cases provided for in Section 58 of this law.
